### [2026-06-26] Process observations (PR #56 post-merge)

**Origin**: `/code-review` pass on PR #56 (Group P3 — lazy / on-demand feature-extraction timing; **merged** into `main` in `9d65500`). Five-agent review + per-issue confidence scoring surfaced 1 finding scored 100 and 2 scored 25 — all three the same root cause (the PR removed two `kickoffBackgroundExtractionIfEnabled()` call sites and left stale references behind) and all **fixed in-branch pre-merge in `cba5352`** before merge. The review produced no unresolved code finding; the two items below are process/tooling improvements to prevent recurrence (mirrors the [2026-04-30] PR #32 process-observations precedent). All Claude-surfaced.

- [x] **No automated gate runs E2E, so a behaviour-change PR can land a silently-broken E2E test** — ✅ **done 2026-07-10 (CW-P, item 1)**: chose mitigation (a) — a Husky **pre-push** hook (`scripts/check-e2e-needed.js` pure classifier + fail-safe CLI + `.husky/pre-push`) that runs the full E2E suite on any runtime-code push and skips docs-only pushes. (b) CI + (c) checklist-only rejected — see the spec Non-goals. — The scored-100 finding was the `clip-graceful-degradation.test.js` "toggling CLIP on … kicks off extraction" test still asserting the removed toggle-on kickoff (`expect(result.calls).toBe(1)`); it would fail on the next `npm run test:e2e` but was invisible to every gate, because the Husky pre-commit hook runs only unit tests (`npx vitest run`) — per [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) "E2E is NOT run by the hook" — and the repo has no CI (`.github/workflows/` absent). It was caught only by the review's git-history agent, not by tooling. Mitigations (pick one): (a) a Husky **pre-push** hook running the E2E suite (or just the changed E2E files); (b) minimal GitHub Actions CI running `npm test` + `npm run test:e2e` on PRs (would also subsume the [2026-06-29-area] no-console CI-strip and [Test E2E suite on Unix/macOS] items already in this file); (c) lowest-effort — a WORKFLOW / pre-merge-checklist rule: "when a PR changes behaviour covered by an E2E test, run `npm run test:e2e` before merge." Note (a)/(b) make E2E runtime + flakiness a gating cost (Electron launch is seconds/test, `workers:1`, `fullyParallel:false`). Effort: S (checklist) / M (pre-push) / M-L (CI). Affected: `.husky/` (new `pre-push`), `.github/workflows/` (new), or [WORKFLOW.md] / pre-merge checklist docs.
- [x] **Adopt a "sweep references when removing a named call site" convention** — ✅ **done 2026-07-10 (CW-P, item 3)**: one CLAUDE.md Best-Practices bullet (grep tests + comments, not just live callers, when removing a named call site). The named `receiving-code-review`/`verification-before-completion` checklists are third-party superpowers plugin skills (not in-repo) → CLAUDE.md is the in-repo home. — All three PR #56 findings (1 E2E assertion + 2 `media-viewer.js` code comments) were stale references to the two removed `kickoffBackgroundExtractionIfEnabled()` call sites; the author's fix commit (`cba5352`) confirmed them with a repo-wide grep only after review. A cheap edit-time check would have caught all three before review: when deleting or relocating a named function/handler call site, grep the repo for the symbol across **tests and comments** (not just live callers) and update or delete each hit. Add this as a step to the `receiving-code-review` / `verification-before-completion` checklist, or a tiny `scripts/` grep helper run before committing a call-site removal. Effort: XS (checklist) / S (script). Affected: pre-merge checklist docs (or a new `scripts/` helper).

### [2026-06-11] Group D security-audit follow-up (preventive tooling)

**Origin**: Referred out of the Group D security & privacy audit (the audit was explicitly scoped as one-time verification, no preventive tooling). The audit confirmed the repo is currently clean; this item is about keeping it that way as the project grows.

- [ ] **Add a pre-commit secret guard (or gitleaks) so credentials can't be committed** — ✅ **tier (a) DONE 2026-06-17 (CW-4)** — `scripts/check-secrets.js` (pure `scanForSecrets` 5 markers + `extractAddedLines` diff parser + CLI) wired first into `.husky/pre-commit`, no new dependency; audit §1/§2 re-run clean. **tier (b) gitleaks still open** (deferred backstop). **tier-a→b coverage delta** (informed deferral, not oversight): the regex guard intentionally does NOT detect fine-grained `github_pat_…` PATs or 40-char AWS _secret_ access keys (only AWS access key _IDs_) — those are what gitleaks would add. [original:] The 2026-06-11 audit was a manual point-in-time check; nothing prevents a future accidental commit of an API key / token / `.env` value. Two tiers: (a) low-effort — extend the existing Husky pre-commit hook with a regex scan of staged content for the high-signal markers used in the audit (`AKIA`, `ghp_`, `xox[baprs]-`, `AIza…`, `BEGIN …PRIVATE KEY`), no new dependency; (b) thorough — integrate [gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks) into pre-commit and/or CI with a config file. Re-run the audit's section-1/2 command blocks to validate. Effort: S (a) / M (b). Affected: `.husky/pre-commit`, `package.json` (lint-staged), possibly a new `.gitleaks.toml`.
- [ ] **Decide on commit-author PII (real name + email in every commit) — accepted risk, revisit before any public release** — The audit (§4) recorded that all commits are authored as `Alexey Minakov <alexminak32@gmail.com>` / `GoodAlex223 <alexminak32@gmail.com>`; this real name + email is baked into every commit SHA and is already public on the GitHub remote. Left as **accepted risk** in the non-destructive audit because remediation requires rewriting all history (`git filter-repo --mailmap` + force-push), which changes every SHA, breaks existing clones and merged-PR references, and is only meaningful paired with a GitHub-side scrub. Tracked here so the decision is revisited deliberately (e.g. before open-sourcing or a public release) rather than staying buried in the audit doc. No action unless the user opts into a history rewrite. Effort: M (if pursued). Affected: full git history, GitHub remote.

### [2026-08-30] From: G4 `pr-review-toolkit` trial (blind re-review of G1's diff) (8 items)

**Origin**: Group G4's adopt-trial of `pr-review-toolkit@claude-plugins-official` (REVIEW-QUEUE.md § 5 Adopt trials). Its four relevant agents (code-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter, pr-test-analyzer, comment-analyzer) were run **blind** — no baseline shown to them — against the exact revision the 2026-08-29 `/code-review` pass reviewed (`git diff 29636fd 9aa27d1`, in a throwaway worktree), so the two finding-lists are comparable on identical code. The items below are the ones **beyond** that baseline which I then **re-verified myself against current `main`**: the agents supplied the lead, the line numbers and the failure reasoning below are my own reads of the code. Claude-surfaced → 🟤. ⚠️ **Re-reviewed 2026-08-30** — a code review of the G4 commit re-derived all eight from source: items **2, 5, 6 and 8 confirmed exactly as filed**; items **1, 3, 4 and 7 stand as defects but are corrected in place** (marked inline). The corrections share one shape, recorded in [REVIEW-QUEUE.md](REVIEW-QUEUE.md) § 5: every claim that failed did so in the same direction — a real defect paired with a confident, unverified *reachability* story. ⚠️ Items 1–4 are lifecycle / shared-state defects, rated per the standing rule by what they can corrupt rather than by how hard they are to trigger. Already-tracked and therefore **not** re-filed: `scoreComplete` with `scores: null` never settling the window (🟤 [2026-08-29] G1 closeout item 1) — three of the four agents independently re-found it, which is corroboration, not a new item.

- [ ] **`moveComparePair` arms `pendingCompareUpdates = 2` while discarding both post results — violating the invariant G1 itself wrote into CLAUDE.md** — [media-viewer.js:5362-5366](../../media-viewer.js#L5362) calls `updateMlModelWithFeatures(...)` twice and throws both return values away; [media-viewer.js:5424](../../media-viewer.js#L5424) then hardcodes `this.pendingCompareUpdates = 2`. The arm's guard (`mlSortedCompare && primaryFeatures && secondaryFeatures`, L5413) checks that *features* exist — never that the *worker* accepted them — but `updateMlModelWithFeatures` returns `false` whenever `!isMlEnabled || !mlWorker`. ⚠️ **Trigger corrected 2026-08-30 (review of this commit).** The originally-filed trigger — "ML toggled off while `isSortedByPrediction && isCompareMode` still hold" — is **unreachable**: the feature-capture block at [media-viewer.js:5188](../../media-viewer.js#L5188) is itself gated on `if (this.isMlEnabled && this.mlWorker)`, so with ML off `leftFeatures`/`rightFeatures` stay `null`, the L5413 guard is false, and control reaches the `else { await this.showMedia(); }` fallthrough. The guard this entry criticises is precisely what rescues that case. The **reachable** path is a TOCTOU: features are captured while ML is on, then `isMlEnabled`/`mlWorker` is cleared *during* the awaited `checkFolderExists`/`createFolder`/`moveFile` IPC that sits between the capture and the posts at L5362-5366 — by the Settings toggle, or by `mlWorker.onerror` itself (item 4) — after which the locals are still truthy, the guard passes, and **nothing is posted**: the counter arms at 2, never reaches 0, and the rating freezes navigation for the full 3 s fallback before rendering stale scores. A *partial* miscount is impossible — both calls read the same `isMlEnabled`/`mlWorker`, so the count is either right (2) or wholly wrong (0). CLAUDE.md L157 states this exact rule ("`n` MUST be the count of worker messages actually posted … or the counter never reaches 0") and describes this site as arming "the same protocol with equivalent inline code" — it is not equivalent. `applyBulkRating` ([media-viewer.js:8186](../../media-viewer.js#L8186)) does it correctly and is the model: count the `true` returns, then `posted > 0 ? _beginDeferredCompareRefresh(posted) : await showMedia()`. **Found independently by three of the four agents** — but convergence on the *mechanism* only; all three shared the unreachable trigger above, so agreement among reviewers is not evidence of reachability. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) `moveComparePair`, [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) L157.
- [ ] **`loadFolder`'s two failure exits still leak a window armed *during* the scan** — G1 added a cancel **before** the scan ([media-viewer.js:2565](../../media-viewer.js#L2565)) and one before the empty/non-empty split ([media-viewer.js:2596](../../media-viewer.js#L2596)). The pre-await call covers a window armed *before* the load; the post-await call covers one armed *during* it — but the `!result.success` early return ([media-viewer.js:2572-2576](../../media-viewer.js#L2572)) and the `catch` ([media-viewer.js:2646-2651](../../media-viewer.js#L2646)) both end the method **before** L2596 ever runs (the `catch` has no literal `return` — it simply falls off the end). Since `showLoadingSpinner()` deliberately does not set `isLoading`, the old folder stays interactive throughout the scan, so a bulk rating there arms a fresh window; if the load then fails or throws, that window survives and 3 s later its fallback calls `showMedia()` against the still-populated old `mediaFiles` — rendering the previous folder's compare pair **on top of the drop zone the error just raised**. This is the residual of the very race G1's review round narrowed, on the failure paths only, and no test covers either exit (both new `loadFolder` tests use `{success: true}`). Fix: hoist the second cancel into a `finally`, or repeat it in both exits. ⚠️ The existing source-order test at [tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js:2488-2501](../../tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js#L2488) asserts there are **exactly two** cancel calls (`expect(body.indexOf(call, second + 1)).toBe(-1)`), so it will fail on the fix and must be updated alongside it. **Confirmed unchanged by the 2026-08-30 review** — do not soften or drop this item. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) `loadFolder`.
- [ ] **`_applyModeSwitch` never cancels an open deferred compare-refresh window** — the only three callers of `_cancelDeferredCompareRefresh()` are the two in `loadFolder` and one in `moveComparePair` ([:2565](../../media-viewer.js#L2565), [:2596](../../media-viewer.js#L2596), [:5388](../../media-viewer.js#L5388)); [media-viewer.js:4279](../../media-viewer.js#L4279) `_applyModeSwitch` calls `switchToSingleModeUI()` then `showMedia()` / `enterTournamentMode()` with no cancel. Rate a pair, then switch mode within 3 s: **compare → single** leaves `mediaNavigationInProgress` held, so the switch's own `showMedia()` early-returns and the user sees an empty media area until the fallback fires; **compare → tournament** renders the tournament pair, then the orphaned fallback calls `showMedia()` on top of it. ⚠️ **Symptom corrected 2026-08-30 (review of this commit):** because `showTournamentPair()` deliberately sets `isCompareMode = true` to reuse the compare wrappers ([media-viewer.js:4734](../../media-viewer.js#L4734)), that `showMedia()` dispatches to `showCompareMedia()`, **not** `showSingleMedia()` — so the corruption is a **wrong-pair re-render under tournament chrome** (the pair comes from `mediaFiles`/`currentIndex` and never consults `tournament.engine.getCurrentPair()`), not the stray single-file render originally filed. The structural facts — three callers, no cancel in `_applyModeSwitch`, the `showMedia()` guard, the unguarded mode-selector click handler — were all confirmed exact. Pre-existing, but G1 centralized this teardown precisely so every exit could use it, and this is the obvious remaining door — the PR added the new consumer whose correctness depends on it. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) `_applyModeSwitch`.
- [ ] **`mlWorker.onerror` is the one site that can *prove* the replies will never arrive, and it does not cancel the window** — [media-viewer.js:6652-6660](../../media-viewer.js#L6652) sets `isMlEnabled = false` and rejects the pending `runMlSort` promise, but leaves `pendingCompareRefresh` / `pendingCompareUpdates` / the 3 s timer / `mediaNavigationInProgress` armed, so a crash mid-rating costs a guaranteed 3 s freeze and then a stale-score render. It also reports only through `console.error`, and the user is never told that ratings have silently stopped training the model (`isMlEnabled` is false from then on). Add the cancel and a `showError(...)`. ⚠️ **Claim retracted 2026-08-30 (review of this commit):** the entry originally added that a worker crash "never reaches the main-process file logger, contrary to the documented renderer-error pattern" — **refuted**. `window.onerror` is registered at [media-viewer.js:237-240](../../media-viewer.js#L237) and forwards to `logError`, and this handler never calls `preventDefault()`, so the error does propagate there; CLAUDE.md L128 lists `window.onerror` as one of the forwarding channels, making the behaviour *consistent* with the documented pattern rather than contrary to it. Everything else here was confirmed exact: no cancel, the guaranteed 3 s freeze, no user notice. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) `initializeMlWorker`.
- [ ] **CLAUDE.md L144 still overstates `restoreFeatureCachesFromHistory`'s call sites** — G1's review round corrected the "before `showMedia()`" half of this bullet to "before the next render" but left "Called in **all** `handleCancel` branches". Verified false: the bulk-rating undo branch ([media-viewer.js:3963-3979](../../media-viewer.js#L3963)) calls `undoBulkRating` and the deferred protocol, never `restoreFeatureCachesFromHistory` — correctly, since `applyBulkRating` moves no files. Inert today, but it is a **live doc asserting a false invariant**, and the next person adding state to a history entry will trust it. Reword to "every `handleCancel` branch that reverses a file move (and tournament undo)". A textbook instance of the late-correction-doesn't-sweep-back class this repo keeps hitting: the fix corrected one half of a sentence and left the other. Effort: XS. Affected: [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) L144.
- [ ] **Three inaccurate comments in the new compare-mode E2E** — (a) the 576-dim override is justified with "a short vector would score NaN", which cannot happen: `OnlineLogisticRegression.predict` reads `features[i] || 0` across `featureDim` ([ml-model.js:52](../../ml-model.js#L52)) and `update` does the same, so a 64-dim vector scores finite with the trailing 512 dims treated as zero — the real reason is that 576 matches `DEFAULT_FEATURE_DIM` so the vector carries signal; (b) `seedLocalStorage(page, {mlPredictionEnabled: 'true'})` runs after `MediaViewer` is constructed and the helper syncs only the three custom-folder keys, so it is a no-op that the explicit `mv.isMlEnabled = true` is silently covering for; (c) the comment claiming `expect(settled.timeout).toBeNull()` discriminates a fallback settle from a reply settle is wrong — the fallback nulls that field before calling `showMedia()`, so the assertion holds on both paths and is also true when no window was ever armed; the load-bearing assertion is the `toEqual` event sequence beside it. Each will mislead the next person to debug this file. Effort: XS (comments only). Affected: [tests/e2e/compare-mode.test.js](../../tests/e2e/compare-mode.test.js).
- [ ] **The `prunedPairKeys` capture tests assert source position, not behavior — and restore is covered on 2 of 5 undo branches** — the capture test for `moveToSpecialFolder` / `moveComparePair` only checks that every `this._bulkPairKeysReferencing(` occurrence precedes the first `this.removeFileFromList(` and that the literal `prunedPairKeys` appears somewhere in the body, so these mutations stay green: passing the wrong file's name into the capture, assigning the captured keys onto the wrong history entry, assigning `[]` under the `length > 0` guard, or capturing correctly and never pushing the entry. The LIFO test hand-writes the same three lines of capture glue the move sites contain, so it asserts the ordering against the test's own code rather than production's (`moveCurrentFile` is the one site with a real behavioral test). Separately — ⚠️ **coverage figure corrected 2026-08-30**, first by review ("2 of 4") and then by a re-count here that found a branch **both** had missed. `restoreFeatureCachesFromHistory` has **five** call branches and the pair-key restore is exercised on **two**. Covered: the single-mode branch ([:4208](../../media-viewer.js#L4208)), and the compare-mode compare-pair branch ([:4092-4093](../../media-viewer.js#L4092)) via _"compare-pair undo (two entries) restores the keys captured on BOTH entries, shared key included"_ ([tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js:2731](../../tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js#L2731)) — a behavioral test added by G1's own review round (`9a86c2e`), which this entry originally listed as uncovered. Genuinely uncovered: the compare-mode **special** branch ([:4033](../../media-viewer.js#L4033)); the `!isCompareMode` compare-pair branch ([:4166-4167](../../media-viewer.js#L4166)) — undoing a pair after leaving compare mode, missed by the original entry *and* by the review, which is why the denominator is 5 and not 4; and `handleTournamentUndo` ([:4950](../../media-viewer.js#L4950)), where the method is `vi.fn()`-mocked so only the call is asserted. The margin note was half-wrong too: the restore-**twice** idempotence *is* asserted (that test's `shared key included`); what nothing asserts is that `moveComparePair`'s capture is what produces the double — the test hand-writes the doubled keys. Effort: M. Affected: [tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js](../../tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js).
- [ ] **The new E2E's stubbed `getCombinedFeatures` hashes the full path, which contains a random temp dir — the rendered pair differs run to run** — `createTempFixtureDir` builds `mv-e2e-XXXXXX` via `mkdtemp`, so hashing `file.path` makes the 576-dim vectors, and therefore `predictionScores`, the `computeAllComparePairs` ordering, and *which two files the deferred `showMedia()` renders*, nondeterministic across runs. With `tiny.mp4` in the fixture set some runs render a video pane (whose nav flag clears only from `onLoadedMetadata`) and some render two 1×1 PNGs — a different timing profile every run, inside the file's heaviest test against a 30 s budget. Hashing `file.name` instead makes it deterministic. Related margin note worth a comment rather than a fix: the undo assertion works because `applyBulkRating` moves `positiveCount` 3→5 and `undoBulkRating` reverses 5→3, landing exactly on `hasEnoughSamples()`'s `>= 3` boundary — one warm-up sample fewer flips `scoreComplete:scores` to `scoreComplete:null` and the test fails for a reason unrelated to the protocol. Effort: S. Affected: [tests/e2e/compare-mode.test.js](../../tests/e2e/compare-mode.test.js).

### [2026-08-29] From: G1 closeout (bulk-rate follow-ups) (4 items)

**Origin**: Closeout of Group G1 (WEEKLY Aug 31–Sep 4 🏆; branch `g1-bulk-rate-followups`, **merged to `main` 2026-08-29 locally as `66b16af`, `--no-ff`, no PR** by task-brief instruction — reviewed locally on the whole branch: 3 findings @75 + 1 sub-threshold, all fixed in `9a86c2e`). The first two were surfaced while designing G1 and **deliberately left unfixed** (spec D5 — outside the approved scope); the last two are the residue of the review round. Claude-surfaced.

- [ ] **`scoreComplete` with `scores: null` does not clear the deferred compare window** — the clear in `handleMlWorkerMessage`'s `scoreComplete` case sits INSIDE `if (message.scores)`, so a null reply (model not ready — `hasEnoughSamples` needs ≥3 likes AND ≥3 dislikes — or scoring cancelled) leaves `pendingCompareRefresh` + `mediaNavigationInProgress` held until the 3 s fallback fires. Reachable in production: bulk-rate a pair before the model is warm. Fix: on a null-scores reply run the same teardown the fallback does (and render). Effort: XS. Affected: `media-viewer.js` (`handleMlWorkerMessage` → `scoreComplete`), `tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js`.
- [ ] **Late `scoreComplete` after a folder switch can write old scores onto same-named files in the new folder** — the `scoreAll` reply is filename-keyed while `predictionScores` is written against the current `mediaFiles`; a reply in flight across `loadFolder()` lands after the array is swapped. `resetMlModel()` on folder change nulls model state but does not discard in-flight replies. Fix: a scoring run-id on `scoreAll`, echoed by the worker and dropped in `scoreComplete` when stale (the `sortRunId` pattern). Effort: S. Affected: `media-viewer.js` (`requestPredictionScores`, `handleMlWorkerMessage`), `ml-worker.js` (echo the id).
- [ ] **`moveToSpecialFolder` / `moveComparePair` `prunedPairKeys` capture sites are covered by a source-order assertion only** — both are dialog/DOM-heavy to extract, so G1 T3 asserted the capture-before-prune ORDER from source text; only `moveCurrentFile` and the two `handleCancel` restore branches (single-file and compare-pair) have behavioral tests. The branch's own thesis is that structural coverage is what let PR #66's D2 ship unverified. Effort: S. Affected: `tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js`.
- [ ] **`mediaNavigationInProgress` doubles as the navigation mutex AND the deferred-window flag — replace the flag half with a per-window token** — `_cancelDeferredCompareRefresh`'s `wasPending` rule and `loadFolder`'s TWO cancel calls (pre-scan because a >3 s scan outlives the fallback; post-scan because `showLoadingSpinner()` does not set `isLoading`, so the old folder can arm a fresh window mid-scan) exist only because the flag has two owners. A token checked by the fallback and by `scoreComplete` would let `loadFolder` cancel once and make a stale window inert rather than cancelled. Folds with the `scoreAll` run-id above. Effort: S-M. Affected: `media-viewer.js` (`_beginDeferredCompareRefresh`, `_cancelDeferredCompareRefresh`, `loadFolder`, `scoreComplete`).

### [2026-08-27] From: G4 closeout (strategic-doc refresh) (4 items)

**Origin**: Closeout of Group G4 (strategic-doc refresh + CLAUDE.md pre-push-gate sync; **merged to `main` 2026-08-27 locally as `a843d36`, no PR** by task-brief instruction — reviewed locally on the whole branch instead). Claude-surfaced. _(`BACKLOG.md` "docs/README.md Last Updated footer chronically stale" is deliberately **not** re-filed and stays open — G4 fixed one instance, which is not the guard the entry asks for.)_

- [ ] **Two dead `2025-12-29_video-fullscreen-toggle.md` rows list an archived plan as active** — the plan lives at `docs/archive/plans/2025-12-29_video-fullscreen-toggle.md`, but two indexes still present it as in-flight: [docs/README.md](../README.md) § Active Plans (a **broken link** — the `planning/plans/` target does not exist) and [docs/planning/plans/README.md](plans/README.md) § Current Plans (row reads `In Progress | 2025-12-29`). Surfaced by G4's link-target sweep and deliberately left unfixed — out of that branch's approved scope, and the fix carries a real choice: move each row to the Archived/Completed table vs. merely repoint the path. Both should be resolved together. Effort: XS. Affected: [docs/README.md](../README.md), [docs/planning/plans/README.md](plans/README.md).
- [ ] **A spec's file list can silently narrow the governing propagation rule — audit it before executing** — G4's spec enumerated five files to edit and omitted `PROJECT.md`, which carried the _exact_ claims the refresh existed to kill (`Testing | Manual (no automated tests)`, `# Run tests (not configured)`, `~6100+ lines`, `No automated tests: Manual testing only`) plus two the review never listed (`No linting configured`, `No pre-commit hooks configured`). Global `CLAUDE.md` § Task Completion names `PROJECT.md` explicitly as a live surface, and Knowledge Sources reads it at **step 2, before `docs/`** — so the stale copy sat in the _higher-priority_ source while the branch fixed only the lower one. Caught by external review, not by the plan. Proposal: add a preflight step to the plan template — _"list every live surface asserting the claims this task corrects (`CLAUDE.md`, `PROJECT.md`, `README`s, prompts, templates), then diff that list against the spec's file list."_ `[possible-dup-of: the derived-checklist-vs-governing-rule pattern already in memory]` — that memory is a personal-workflow note; this asks for a repo-level template step. Effort: S. Affected: `.claude/TEMPLATES/` (plan template), [docs/planning/plans/README.md](plans/README.md).
- [ ] **Never enumerate a living table's range in another document (G4 spec D12)** — the same defect fired **three times in one branch**: `baed3ba` wrote "D6–D9" while D10 was pending; `02998ec` corrected it to "D6–D10" _while adding D11 in the same diff_; restating it as "D6–D11" would only have reset the clock. Fixed by deleting the derived value ("§ 9 records … **D6 onward**") rather than refreshing it. The pattern generalizes past G4 — this repo's docs carry many derived counts (test totals, `N items` in BACKLOG group headers, "2 of 6 managers", Health-Summary tallies), each of which goes stale the moment its source grows. Proposal: a convention line in [docs/planning/README.md](README.md) — _"do not restate a count or range that is derived from a list elsewhere; reference the list open-endedly"_ — plus a sweep of existing `(N items)` headers to confirm they still match. **Review-time tell** worth codifying: when checking a fix, verify whether the _same commit_ extended the source the fix just described. Effort: S (convention) / M (sweep). Affected: [docs/planning/README.md](README.md), BACKLOG group headers.
- [ ] **The editor reformats Markdown on save, independently of `.prettierignore`** — during G4, files reflowed on disk seconds after being written (table padding, `*emphasis*`→`_emphasis_`, rewrapping), even though `.prettierignore` lists `docs/` and `*.md` and `lint-staged` only formats `*.{js,cjs,json,css,html}` — so **repo tooling is not the cause**; it is an editor/IDE format-on-save acting on files merely _opened_. Twice this had real consequences: a `git add -A` swept six unrelated planning docs into a G4 commit (reverted in `900a6ea`), and among that noise the formatter had inserted **backslash escapes inside code spans** — `` `scripts/**/*.js` `` → `` `scripts/\*\*/*.js` ``, which renders the escapes literally — plus `\*setupAutoHide` and `(tests/\**/*.test.js)` in DONE.md. Corrupting, not merely cosmetic. Proposal: settle it at the repo level so it does not depend on one machine's settings — add `.vscode/settings.json` disabling `editor.formatOnSave` for `[markdown]`, and/or a pre-commit guard rejecting staged `.md` hunks whose only change is whitespace/escape churn. Interim rule already adopted: stage docs commits **by explicit path**, never `git add -A`. Effort: S. Affected: `.vscode/settings.json` (new), `scripts/`, `.husky/pre-commit`.

### [2026-08-27] From: G5 closeout whole-branch review (5 reviewers) (2 items)

**Origin**: Local 5-reviewer whole-branch review of `chore/g5-weekly-reviews` before the user-directed
local merge (`4f1e65a`, no PR). 2 findings at threshold + 5 sub-threshold, all folded in `e4f6967`.
These two are the _generalizations_ of the threshold findings, not the fixes themselves (those shipped).
Claude/automation-surfaced.

- [ ] **Derive a closeout's artifact list from the Task-Completion rule instead of hand-writing it** — both threshold findings had the same root cause: the run-card's own **Outputs** list never named `DONE.md` or `docs/README.md`, so the closeout followed the list and silently skipped two globally-mandated steps. A hand-written Outputs list gets executed _instead of_ the rule it implements, and its omissions read as completeness — they never reach the deviations ledger, because a ledger records _chosen_ departures. Proposal: give the run-card / plan template a fixed **Closeout artifacts** block enumerating every artifact the global CLAUDE.md Task-Completion step names (BACKLOG, TODO, DONE, WEEKLY, `docs/README.md`, `docs/archive/plans/`), each marked done or **N/A with a reason** — an explicit N/A is auditable, an omission is not. Detection heuristics worth encoding: a "closeout" commit touching fewer artifacts than the rule names, and a deviations ledger with no entry for a skipped step. `[possible-dup-of: 🟤 [2026-07-04] Automate the pre-archive "flip all `- [ ]`→`- [x]`" check]` — that one validates checkbox state _inside_ an archived plan; this one validates that the closeout _touched every mandated artifact_. Files: `.claude/TEMPLATES/`, the Weekly-Reviews methodology section of [`2026-06-26-weekly-reviews-first-run-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-06-26-weekly-reviews-first-run-design.md), global `CLAUDE.md` Task Completion.
- [ ] **Automate the `docs/README.md` index check — the manual checklist has now failed 7 times** — "new documentation → index in `docs/README.md`" is a documented recurring class: filed and scored 75–100/100 on PRs #19/#23/#27/#28/#39/#43, with **3 still open** (the `clip-extraction-silent-failure`, TASK-026 and TASK-024 backfills below), and it fired again in this run on **both** the 2026-08-27 run-card _and_ the 2026-07-05 one (the latter missed because PR #62 was waved through as docs-only). Seven manual misses is enough evidence that a checklist is the wrong instrument. Proposal: a dependency-free Node script in the established `scripts/` house pattern (`check-secrets.js`, `check-e2e-needed.js`) that fails when a file under `docs/superpowers/specs/` or `docs/archive/plans/` has no link definition in `docs/README.md`; wire it into the pre-commit chain (cheap, no network) or pre-push. `[possible-dup-of: 🟤 [2026-05-06] Index 2026-05-06-clip-extraction-silent-failure-design.md; 🟤 [2026-03-27] docs/README.md not updated for TASK-026; 🟤 [2026-03-24] Index TASK-024 spec]` — those three are one-off backfills; this is the guard that closes all three and prevents the next one. Files: `scripts/`, `.husky/pre-commit`, `package.json` (lint-staged), `tests/`.

### [2026-08-27] From: Weekly Reviews (2026-08-27 catch-up run) (3 items)

**Origin**: Group G5 Weekly Reviews, 3rd run — merged `4f1e65a` (no PR), run-card [`2026-08-27-weekly-reviews-run.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-weekly-reviews-run.md), verdicts in [REVIEW-QUEUE.md](REVIEW-QUEUE.md). All three are `adopt` verdicts and therefore **hands-off trial entries** (D4): the run installs nothing and changes no project behavior. Claude/review-surfaced.

- [x] **Trial the `security-guidance` official plugin (Plugins / store)** — Install `security-guidance@claude-plugins-official` and trial it. Three layers: a **free** deterministic per-edit pattern match (no model call), a background end-of-turn diff review, and an agentic review on each commit/push Claude makes. Fit is concrete rather than generic: the built-in DOM-injection patterns (`.innerHTML =`, `document.write`) land on the ~8400-line renderer; `child_process.exec` lands on the ffprobe/ffmpeg main-process calls; and `.claude/security-patterns.yaml` `paths` globs can make CLAUDE.md's advisory _"changes to preload.js require security review"_ **deterministic**, with `.claude/claude-security-guidance.md` carrying the Electron threat model (`sandbox:false`, contextIsolation, the IPC bridge, file move/delete ops). This **reverses** the plugin's own [2026-07-05] "low fit" parking — that note reasoned "no CI + already gated by ESLint + the secret guard", but the plugin occupies the _in-session_ stage neither covers, and having **no CI** is an argument for it (no later net), not against. **Trial-eval points (why a trial, not a blind adopt)**: needs **Python 3.10+ on `PATH`** plus a first-run venv at `~/.claude/security/` requiring pip + network — _was unverified on this Windows/Node box; **measured 2026-08-30 — Python 3.13.5 on `PATH`, venv present at `~/.claude/security/`, win32 fine**_; the model-backed layers cost ~1 review per file-changing turn + 1 deeper review per commit, on **Opus 4.7 by default** (`SECURITY_REVIEW_MODEL` / `SG_AGENTIC_MODEL` to downgrade); and the commit layer only sees commits **Claude** makes via Bash, not the user's own shell commits — which is how most commits happen here. Source: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/security-guidance. Effort: S (evaluation only — install + trial; optionally two `.claude/` rule files). Affected: evaluation only (Claude Code plugin config; optionally `.claude/security-patterns.yaml`, `.claude/claude-security-guidance.md`).
  ✅ **Trial closed 2026-08-30 (Group G4) — outcome: keep.** No install was needed: it was **already installed at user scope on 2026-08-24 and enabled**, i.e. three days *before* the 2026-08-27 run that adopted it. The prerequisite this entry flags as unverified is now **measured on this box**: Python **3.13.5** on `PATH` (`python`/`python3`/`py` all resolve), venv built at `~/.claude/security/`, win32 fine — 56 session-state files, ~1.9 MB of hook logs, and observed firing on `PostToolUse` during the G4 session itself. Still unmeasured: the token cost of the model-backed layers, and it only sees commits Claude makes via Bash. Row in [REVIEW-QUEUE.md](REVIEW-QUEUE.md) § 5.
- [ ] **Trial the `dead-rules-audit` plugin (Plugins / wider internet)** — Install from `karanb192/claude-code-hooks` (MIT, 488★, **Node ≥18, no npm dependencies**, platform-agnostic — no win32 blocker) and trial its CLAUDE.md **compliance scorecard**: it parses rules at `SessionStart`, scores every `Edit`/`Write` in `PostToolUse`, and at `SessionEnd` renders a worst-first scorecard flagging chronically-ignored rules `⚠ promote→hook` (also on demand via `/dead-rules-audit:scorecard`). The fit was **measured, not assumed** — run with the plugin's own parser, this repo's `CLAUDE.md` yields **36 rules / 10 judgeable** _(superseded — first-hand re-measurement in the trial-vehicle note below: **38 / 11**)_, parsing whole atomic rules, where the sibling config repo's hard-wrapped prose yielded 5 rules / 2 mid-sentence fragments. It targets this repo's best-documented chronic failure: advisory rules that keep being ignored — the doc-drift class recurring across PR #51 → #52 → #63 and **still open** as G4's 🟤, and the "grep the repo for the symbol" rule whose breach caused the PR #56 follow-ups. The repo already does that promotion **by hand** (pre-commit secret guard, pre-push E2E gate) with no signal for _which_ rule to promote next. **Trial-eval points**: only 11 of 38 rules are judgeable (prohibition-shaped; re-measured 2026-08-30 — see the trial-vehicle note), so the scorecard is a partial view; scoring is token-presence heuristics, not semantic compliance, so its "violations" must be read rather than obeyed; and it adds `SessionStart`/`PostToolUse`/`SessionEnd` hook overhead on every edit. **Also closes an inbound propagation item** — routed here by `claude-code-universal-config` (user-confirmed 2026-07-27) and deliberately not double-filed; see [REVIEW-QUEUE.md](REVIEW-QUEUE.md) §4 inbound. Source: https://github.com/karanb192/claude-code-hooks. Effort: S (evaluation only). Affected: evaluation only (Claude Code plugin config).
  **Trial vehicle (set 2026-08-30, Group G4)**: installed 2026-08-30 (marketplace `karanb192/claude-code-hooks` added, enabled at project scope); it rides the **G2 + G3 implementation sessions (Sep 1–3)**, both edit-heavy, and the scorecard is read out at **G6 on Fri Sep 4**. A docs-only session would measure its floor, not its fit. Row in [REVIEW-QUEUE.md](REVIEW-QUEUE.md) § 5. **Parser re-measured first-hand 2026-08-30** (its own `parseRules` run against this repo's `CLAUDE.md`): **38 rules parsed — 12 prohibition-shaped — 11 actually judgeable**, not the 36 / 10 the sibling repo reported (CLAUDE.md is **206** lines). "Judgeable" is narrower than "prohibition": the code scores a rule only if it is a prohibition **and** names a concrete backticked token. **8 of the 11 come from the Active-gotchas section** (the other three sit outside it, at CLAUDE.md L97, L133 and L157) **and 3 are tournament-specific** (`handleTournamentUndo` / `engine.history`, `engine.files` vs `strategy.files`, folder-scoped tournament mode) — which is what makes **G2 the load-bearing half of the vehicle**; G3 (scripts + docs) will barely touch a judgeable rule. ⚠️ **Precondition**: the `PostToolUse` matcher is `Edit|MultiEdit|Write` — **file changes made through Bash are invisible to it**, so a session that edits via shell scores nothing and the scorecard would read as "compliant" when it simply never observed anything. G2/G3 must use the Edit/Write tools for the trial to produce data. **This is not hypothetical: auto mode routes file edits through Bash, and the G4 session that installed the plugin made every one of its own edits that way** — it would have scored zero. Recorded as a callout on the G2 and G3 group headers in [WEEKLY.md](WEEKLY.md), where the sessions will actually meet it, rather than here alone. **Read-out rule**: a scorecard with **zero scored edits is `inconclusive`, never `keep`**.
- [ ] **Migrate path-conditional CLAUDE.md content to path-scoped rules (`.claude/rules/`)** — The Claude Code steering guidance is explicit: _"Keep CLAUDE.md under 200 lines"_, and as it grows, conventions migrate to **path-scoped rules** and procedures to **skills**, "where they load only when needed". Measured here, not assumed: `CLAUDE.md` is **205 lines** and **`.claude/rules/` does not exist**, so every path-conditional block is launch-loaded into every session regardless of relevance. Concrete migration candidates: the Vitest unit-testing conventions (`tests/**`), the E2E Electron-wrapper gotchas (`tests/e2e/**`), the Web-Worker conventions (`*-worker.js`), the preload security note (`preload.js`). The **hook-promotion** and **skills** halves of the same guidance are already practiced here (the pre-commit secret guard and pre-push E2E gate are both promoted rules; skills are in daily use) — path-scoped rules are the one unpracticed third. The sibling `claude-code-universal-config` shipped exactly this migration for itself (branch `feat/g2-path-scoped-rules-and-claude-md-sizing`), so the pattern is proven in-house and can be copied rather than designed. **Supersedes-by-doing** the 🟤 [2026-07-03] "CLAUDE.md 204-line soft-cap overshoot" item — that one asks _trim_, this asks _relocate_, which is the durable fix; resolve them together. Source: https://claude.com/blog/steering-claude-code-skills-hooks-rules-subagents-and-more. Effort: M (content migration + verify per-path loading in a fresh session). Affected: [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md), new `.claude/rules/*.md`. [possible-dup-of: 🟤 [2026-07-03] CLAUDE.md soft-cap overshoot]

### [2026-08-24] PR #66 (G3 — bulk-rate re-pair avoidance) smoke round-1 + review follow-ups (6 items)

**Origin**: Group G3 (branch `feat/g3-bulk-rate-repair-avoidance`) — merged to `main` 2026-08-24 on user direction (GitHub PR #66 was closed unmerged, then merged locally `--no-ff`). ⚠️ **User-side re-smoke round 2 was NOT run** — round 1 (parallel Verification chat) found 2 real defects the 500-unit/55-E2E suite missed (D1 counter shrink-then-jump; D2 rated pairs not re-mixing — synchronous render from pre-rating scores, a pre-existing non-deferral G3 unmasked), both fixed via a deferred-re-render (`pendingCompareRefresh`) rework + a full-extremes-count stable counter. Items below are the deferred residue from that session's SDD ledger + this session's opus whole-branch review. Claude/review-surfaced.

- [x] **Deferred-refresh protocol has ZERO E2E coverage — stub `mlWorker`** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-08-29** (Group G1 T1, merge `66b16af`, no PR) — shipped as the **real** `ml-worker.js`, not a stub: `mlWorker` was null under Playwright because `initializeMlWorker()` is lazy (first AI sort / settings toggle), not a harness limit. The E2E warms the model (3+3) and asserts `updateComplete×2 → scoreComplete:scores → showMedia` for the rating and the reverse chain for its undo; fails against a reverted D2. _Original:_ under Playwright `mlWorker` is null, so `postedUpdates` is always 0 and `_beginDeferredCompareRefresh` is never reached; the compare-mode bulk-rating E2E passes for the WRONG reason (it never exercises the D2 fix — the main correctness property of this branch). Add a worker stub so the deferred path gets real coverage. Effort: M. Affected: `tests/e2e/compare-mode.test.js`, E2E harness.
- [x] **Undo of a single-file move doesn't reinstate the pruned `bulkRatedPairs` key** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-08-29** (Group G1 T3, merge `66b16af`): `_bulkPairKeysReferencing(name)`; the three move sites stash `prunedPairKeys` on the history entry before `removeFileFromList`; `restoreFeatureCachesFromHistory` re-adds them before its `mlFeatures` guard (tournament undo included). _Original:_ `removeFileFromList` prunes any pair key referencing a removed file, but the compare-mode like/dislike/special **undo** paths restore the file (via `_restoredPairFiles` + `restoreFeatureCachesFromHistory`) WITHOUT reinstating the key. After `rate-pair (a,f) → single-rate a → undo`, `key(a,f)` is gone so `(a,f)` can reappear. Session-only, self-healing (re-rate), contrived; no data loss. Fix: capture referencing keys onto the move-history entry, restore alongside the feature caches. Effort: S-M. Affected: `media-viewer.js` (`removeFileFromList`, `handleCancel` restore branches).
- [x] **`loadFolder` doesn't clear `pendingCompareRefresh`/`pendingCompareTimeout`** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-08-29** (Group G1 T2 + review round, merge `66b16af`): `_cancelDeferredCompareRefresh()` called TWICE in `loadFolder` — before the scan (a >3 s scan outlives the fallback) and before the empty/non-empty split (the spinner does not set `isLoading`, so a window can be armed mid-scan). _Original:_ a stray 3 s deferred-refresh timer can fire `showMedia()` against the NEW folder after a folder switch (bulk-rate then immediately switch folders → known, deferred). The cleanup idiom already exists at the `moveComparePair` site. Effort: S. Affected: `media-viewer.js` (`loadFolder`).
- [x] **Coverage gaps in the counter + undo arithmetic** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-08-29** (Group G1 T4, merge `66b16af`): fall-through tested at `mlComparePairIndex = 1` (index 0 cannot see the mutation); `undoBulkRating` posted-count tested with `true/true`, `true/false`, `false/false` and null-feature mocks. _Original:_ (a) `updateNavigationInfo`'s duplicated fall-through branch is UNTESTED (a mutation removing the fall-through survives all 513 tests; shipped code proven correct only by fuzz); (b) `undoBulkRating`'s posted-count arithmetic is untested (all `reverseMlModelUpdate` mocks return `undefined`; the Task-4 test stubs `undoBulkRating` wholesale). Effort: S. Affected: `tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js`.

Effort: S each. Affected: `media-viewer.js`.

O(n log n) each; user-paced but the repo has 24k perf history. Folds with the existing memoization item. Effort: S-M. Affected: `media-viewer.js`.

### [2026-07-21] PR #65 review follow-ups (9 items)

**Origin**: PR #65 (Group G2 — tournament-mode bug fixes; branch `fix/g2-tournament-bug-fixes`) — **merged** into `main` in `937084c` on 2026-07-21 (branch deleted, remote + local). An external `/code-review` posted **"No issues found"** (4 candidates all sub-threshold). Verifying the top candidate's "already tracked in BACKLOG" claim surfaced a real defect the review missed — the `isLoading` mutex is advisory, not owned (see line ~329) — **fixed in-branch pre-merge in `ae98e85`** (identity re-check + rollback), not deferred. The six-agent per-task reviews + an opus whole-branch review had already caught (all fixed in-branch): a TOCTOU race the plan's own code introduced (`1c18029`), an invisible-but-clickable exit button (`0848723`), an uncovered `while` (`db0b41b`), and two false CLAUDE.md assertions (`dbd1bd4`/`ead9a1e`). The items below are the deferred residue. Claude/review-surfaced. **Note: 9 items in one PR group is above the usual cadence — surface as a Cleanup Week trigger if the pending 🟤 tail keeps growing.**

- [ ] **`reconcileWithFiles` still removes files untracked → Tier-0 strand on undo-past** — [tournament.js](../../tournament.js) `reconcileWithFiles` (~L119) calls `engine.removeFile(f)` with no options (`trackUndo:false`), and it runs on **every** tournament-mode entry. Reachable with a non-empty `engine.history`: `exitTournamentMode` does not null the engine and `switchMode` skips the leave prompt once `engine.isComplete()`, so a finish-without-Apply → leave → file-set change → re-enter prunes untracked; a later Ctrl+A crossing that point restores `engine.files` from a stale `filesSnapshot` but not `strategy` state. **Tradeoff — the naive fix is WRONG:** a bulk reconcile can prune hundreds of files at once, each pushing an O(n) `strategy.serialize()` snapshot, which would undo the CW-T/PR #55 24k perf win. Prefer **dropping the session-only `engine.history` when a reconcile prunes anything** (O(1), honest: `peekUndoKind()`→null → button disabled → "Nothing to undo"). CLAUDE.md:191 already documents this gap. Effort: S. Affected: [tournament.js](../../tournament.js), [tournament-engine.js](../../tournament-engine.js).
- [ ] **A failed special-restore can wedge the unified undo stack** — in `handleTournamentUndo`, a `special` entry whose `moveFile` restore fails (disk/permission) hits `showError` and returns with the stack untouched, so the dead entry stays on top and every later Ctrl+A retries the same absent path — no way to skip it. (The _concurrent-mutation_ variant was closed in `ae98e85` via the identity re-check + rollback; this _disk-failure_ variant remains.) Also: `moveHistory` remains independently consumable by `handleCancel` outside tournament mode, which can strand the matching `engine.history` special entry the same way. Consider a defensive drop-or-demote on repeated restore failure. Effort: S-M. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`handleTournamentUndo` special branch).
- [ ] **Document the "`exitTournamentMode` must be paired with nulling `tournament.engine`" invariant** — the safety of the unified undo stack across a mode-switch rests on an _undocumented accidental invariant_: every tournament exit that could leave `engine.history` behind also nulls the engine — except `switchMode`'s `isComplete()` bypass, reachable via Escape-out-of-summary-modal → the modal's own Undo. A future exit path that forgets to null the engine would silently reintroduce a cross-mode undo hazard. Add a comment at `exitTournamentMode`. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js).
- [ ] **Empty-state keydown guard gates the undo _shortcut_ on `moveHistory.length`** — [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (~L2041): when `mediaFiles.length === 0` the undo shortcut only fires if `moveHistory.length > 0`, so a tournament emptied purely via the `-1` auto-prune (no special move) swallows Ctrl+A while `engine.history` still holds an undoable entry — and post-G2 the `#tournamentUndoBtn` can read _enabled_ while the shortcut no-ops. The button _click_ path still works. Pre-existing; G2 makes the mismatch newly visible. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js).
- [ ] **Keyboard-focus a11y: auto-hidden chrome stays tab-focusable + Enter-activatable while invisible** — `opacity:0` does not remove an element from tab order or block keyboard activation (unlike `pointer-events`), so a keyboard-only user can Tab onto `#tournamentExitBtn` while `.tournament-header` is at `opacity:0` — with no focus-ring cue, since the ring paints inside the 0-opacity subtree — and press Enter to open the leave prompt. New behaviour introduced by G2 Task 5, but `.header` has the identical characteristic (D6 mirrors it), so a real fix (`visibility`/`inert` on reveal) must touch `.header` too. Effort: S. Affected: [styles.css](../../styles.css), [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`_setupAutoHide`).
- [ ] **No E2E fires a real `WheelEvent` over a media element to assert zoom still works** — the G2 wheel-guard's zoom-preservation (element listeners bubble before the document handler; no `stopPropagation`) is verified by static trace only; `tests/e2e/zoom.test.js` only drives the popover/slider/Escape UI. A future reorder / added `stopPropagation` / broken `_buildTournamentSide`→`setupZoomEvents` wiring would silently regress zoom-over-media with zero test failure. Effort: S. Affected: [tests/e2e/zoom.test.js](../../tests/e2e/zoom.test.js).
- [ ] **Trailing `await showTournamentPair()` is uncovered by any `isLoading` guard across the tournament-handler family** — `handleTournamentPick`/`handleTournamentDraw`/`handleTournamentUndo` all end with an unguarded `await this.showTournamentPair()`, so two rapid triggers can render concurrently. Systemic/pre-existing; symmetric with the fast-path re-entrancy items. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js). ### [2026-07-13] PR #64 review follow-ups (2 items)

**Origin**: External `/code-review` of PR #64 (Group G1 — AI-sort startup UX & incremental cache-load; branch `feature/g1-ai-sort-startup-ux`, open at review time — since **merged** into `main` in `b6ff4ac` on 2026-07-20). The headline finding (a data-loss regression: a cancelled feature-cache load left a truncated live `featureCache` that the 30s auto-save persisted over the full on-disk `.feature_cache.json`) was **fixed in-branch in `b8b5636`** (build-local, commit-on-complete), not deferred. Two **sub-threshold** items were flagged "worth your judgment" and deferred here with reasoning; both are real but low-frequency. Claude/review-surfaced. _(Also fixed in `b8b5636`, not filed: CLAUDE.md `predictionScores` bullet → `applyPredictionSortResult`; the stale "subarray views" comment → ".slice() copies".)_

- [x] **`loadFolder` doesn't invalidate an in-flight AI sort (cross-folder result apply)** — ✅ **FIXED 2026-07-20 in `c947081`** (deferral was WRONG: the user's 24k smoke hit it immediately, and it turned out to be the _trigger_ for cache corruption — the surviving sort kept extracting against the new folder, producing entries whose metadata couldn't be resolved → `size:0/mtime:0` → permanent staleness → near-empty map auto-saved over a 23,559-entry cache). Added `_abortInFlightPredictionSort()` which bumps `sortRunId`, aborts the controller AND settles the pending `runMlSort` promise; hoisted the teardown trio above `loadFolder`'s empty/non-empty branch split. _Original:_ `loadFolder()` sets `isSortedByPrediction = false` but never bumps `sortRunId` nor aborts `sortAbortController`, unlike the established `extractionRunId` reset. A `runMlSort` pending from the previous folder can resolve after the new folder loads, and `applyPredictionSortResult` would map its `sortedFilenames` against the new folder's files — truncating `mediaFiles` to the filename intersection if names collide. Real but sub-threshold (needs a mid-sort folder switch **plus** colliding filenames) and partly pre-existing (the pre-branch fire-and-forget `sortComplete` could always cross folders). **NON-trivial fix**: bumping `sortRunId` alone is unsafe — a stale `sortComplete` then `break`s without calling `_mlSortResolve`, so `await runMlSort` hangs forever and wedges **both** sort paths (via the mutual-exclusion guard); the fix must also **settle** the pending promise (`_mlSortReject(new Error('folder changed'))`) with careful async ordering, plus a test. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`loadFolder` — add a sort-cancel block near the `isSortedByPrediction = false` reset), `tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js`.
- [ ] **Binary transport skips the dimension guard (`ingest` dead check + clip asymmetry)** — on the binary `feature-cache-chunk` path, `packFeatureChunk` always emits a 64-length slice, so `ingest`'s `vector.length !== expectedDim` guard is unreachable — a malformed on-disk vector (e.g. length 32) gets zero-padded to 64 and **accepted**, where the JSON path rejects it. The sibling `clipVector` **is** validated (`length === 512`) — that asymmetry is the smell. Only reachable via external corruption of our own always-64-dim format (our writer never emits a wrong length), so low value; restoring parity means threading a per-entry `validVec` mask through `packFeatureChunk` → renderer (parallel to `hasClip`) + Task-5 test updates. Effort: XS–S. Affected: [feature-cache-transport.js](../../feature-cache-transport.js) (`packFeatureChunk`), [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`_loadFeatureCacheLocked` binary branch), `tests/feature-cache-transport.test.js`.

### [2026-07-20] G1 24k-smoke failure follow-ups (1 item)

**Origin**: The user-side 24k manual smoke on PR #64 (**merged** into `main` in `b6ff4ac` on 2026-07-20) failed on all three checks and exposed a **real data-loss incident** — a 20,929-file folder's 23,559-entry / 126 MB `.feature_cache.json` was overwritten with 32 entries (31 of them `size:0/mtime:0`). Restored from the user's own `.bak`. Root cause chain and the three fixes are in `2777bdf` (shrink guard + never serialize unresolvable entries) and `c947081` (abort in-flight sort on folder change; cancelable training). This item is the remaining _source-level_ gap, downgraded from destructive to benign by those fixes. Claude-surfaced.

- [ ] **Attribute feature metadata at extraction time instead of via a live `mediaFiles` lookup** — every extraction path stores metadata only `if (fileInfo)` where `fileInfo = this.mediaFiles.find((f) => f.path === …)` (`media-viewer.js` ~1343, ~4978, ~4998, ~7131, ~7899, ~8107). When that lookup fails — the file left the folder mid-extraction (rated/moved), or a stale cross-folder sort completed — `featureMetadata` is silently skipped, so the entry has no size/mtime. **No longer destructive**: `buildEntry` now returns `null` and both writers skip such entries (`2777bdf`), and the folder-switch trigger is gone (`c947081`). Residual cost is only that such a file never caches and silently re-extracts next time. Proper fix: capture `size`/`mtimeMs` from the file object at **enqueue** time and carry them on the pending task, so completion doesn't depend on a live lookup — touches `enqueueFeatureExtraction` + `featurePendingTasks` + the ~6 completion sites. Effort: S–M. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`enqueueFeatureExtraction`, `handleFeatureWorkerMessage`, the extraction completion sites).

### [2026-07-05] Group CW-V (test & tooling backfill) closeout follow-ups (2 items)

**Origin**: Closeout of Group CW-V (test-only backfill — comment-aware `methodSource` guard, real-git `extractAddedLines` fixtures, sort-progress + play/pause E2E). Both Claude-surfaced during the task/final reviews. Test-only, **merged via PR #61** (merge `85f1f29`, 2026-07-05). _(The pre-existing `no-shadow` lint warning on `removeFileFromList` in `tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js` re-surfaced here but is already tracked — CW-1 [2026-06-14] follow-up — so it is NOT re-filed.)_

- [ ] **Extend the `methodSource` guard (or a full span-skipping extractor) when a 2nd brace-bearing caller is added** — CW-V implemented the [2026-06-25] "`methodSource` brace-counting hardening" as a comment-aware guard that flags an unbalanced brace inside a **string/template** span and skips comment contents. It leaves three **accepted, documented residuals** the naive outer counter still miscounts: an unbalanced brace inside a **comment**, inside a **regex literal** (`/…/`), or an **escaped `\{` in a string**. Only caller today is `loadFolder` (brace-safe), so this is YAGNI-deferred — but if a second `methodSource(...)`/`methodSource(name, src)` caller is added whose body hits a residual, promote the guard to a real span-aware extractor (track comment/regex spans + escape semantics) so extraction can't silently mis-slice. The PR #61 `/code-review` additionally surfaced the inverse failure mode: the guard can **FALSE-throw** (loud — never a silent mis-slice) on two balanced constructs it doesn't model — a **nested template** `` `${`…`}` `` (the inner backtick is read as the outer closer while `spanBalance` is nonzero) or a **regex char-class holding a quote** (`/['"]/`, which opens a phantom string span) — documented as accepted in the guard doc-comment in `e737589`. The same span-aware-extractor promotion resolves these too. Effort: S–M. Affected: [tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js](../../tests/media-viewer-utils.test.js) (`assertLiteralBracesBalanced`, `methodSource`). ### [2026-07-03] PR #59 post-merge review (2 items)

**Origin**: Post-merge `/code-review` of PR #59 (Group CW-T — **merged** into `main` in `ae9588d`, 2026-07-03). Five-agent review + confidence scoring flagged **2 real issues** — both scored 75 by the Haiku scorers but **controller-verified real** (issue 1 by direct execution of the branch engine, issue 2 by code-reading), so reported rather than filtered (the 75s were frequency/recoverability-discounted, not realness). Both were **fixed in-branch pre-merge in `f4b7807`** (HIGH delta-undo-across-`removeFile` corruption → snapshot-based `removeFile(file,{trackUndo:true})` at the auto-prune site; close-guard-vs-resume-prompt → guard scoped to the leave-prompt title; +3 unit, 411 total), and the re-review posted "No issues remaining". A 3rd candidate (fast-path re-entrancy race) was dismissed by author + reviewer as not actionable. The 2 items below are the surviving residuals. Claude-surfaced.

- [x] **Special-move → undo-past still leaves `strategy.files` diverged (delta-undo can't resurrect a removed file's strategy state)** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-07-21** (Group G2 Tasks 1-2, PR #65, merge `937084c`): the special-move removal is now `engine.removeFile(path, {trackUndo:true, kind:'special', meta})` on the unified `engine.history` stack, capturing a full `strategy.serialize()` snapshot before mutating; `undoUserAction()` restores `strategy.files`/`byes`/`winCounts`/`roundQueue` (not just `engine.files`), so a restored special file re-pairs and reports its real tier instead of Tier-0. The entangled two-stack-interleaving item (line ~566) is resolved by the same change. _Original:_ The `f4b7807` fix opted `removeFile(file, { trackUndo: true })` in only at the reachable `-1` auto-prune site (reversed via `engine.undo()`). The **special-move** path (`moveToSpecialFolder` → `engine.removeFile(path)`, untracked) is deliberately left on the renderer's dedicated special-undo branch, which restores `engine.files`/`mediaFiles`/caches but **not** `strategy.files`/`byes`/`winCounts` — so undoing back past a delta-captured pick that preceded the special-move can't restore the removed file's strategy state (the pre-PR full-per-pick snapshot could). Leaving it untracked is correct in isolation (a `trackUndo` entry there would desync the renderer's two undo stacks), so the real fix is entangled with the pre-existing [2026-06-24] `handleTournamentUndo` two-stack-interleaving item and should be tackled together. Largely pre-existing (the special branch never restored `strategy.files` on `main` either), but the delta change widens it. Effort: M (needs the two-stack untangle). Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`handleTournamentUndo` special branch ~L4545, `moveToSpecialFolder` ~L1557), [tournament-engine.js](../../tournament-engine.js) (`removeFile`/`undo`). [entangled-with: [2026-06-24] `handleTournamentUndo` two-stack interleaving; from the PR #59 fix scope-note in `f4b7807`]
- [ ] **Tournament fast-path has no re-entrancy guard — an error mid-build can revoke the sibling side's JXL object URL** — `showTournamentPairFast` phase-separates cleanup/build to keep the shared `_jxlObjectURLs` revoke safe _within_ one call, but `_buildTournamentSide`'s JXL-decode-failure `catch` and its media `error` listener both call `showTournamentPair()` fire-and-forget, re-entering `showTournamentPairFast` → `cleanupCompareMedia` → `revokeJxlObjectURLs()` (clears the shared set) while the sibling side's build from the same `Promise.all` may still be in flight → can blank that side. No `isLoading`/generation guard blocks the re-entry. Assessed **low-severity self-healing transient** (the re-entrant render rebuilds both sides) by both author and reviewer, so deferred — a small in-flight/generation token on the fast path would close it. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`showTournamentPairFast`, `_buildTournamentSide`). [possible-dup-of: [2026-07-02] "Tournament fast-path swap doesn't set `isLoading`/`mediaNavigationInProgress`" — same missing-re-entrancy-guard root]

### [2026-06-30] PR #58 post-merge review (2 items)

**Origin**: Post-merge `/code-review` of PR #58 (Group T1) — five-agent review + confidence scoring posted **"No issues found"** (no finding ≥80). The lone scored-75 finding (discard-path fail-safe gap — `discardBtn.onclick` awaited a bare `deleteTournamentState` IPC before `cleanup()`/`onAfterLeave()`, so a rejected delete on the new app-close path skipped `allowAppClose()` and left the window un-closable on that attempt) was **folded in PRE-MERGE in `3ad32bb`** (try/catch around `handleDiscard`, +1 unit test; 389 unit), not deferred. The 2 items below were surfaced while verifying that fix. Claude-surfaced.

- [x] **Best-effort discard can leave stale tournament state on disk** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-07-02** (Group CW-T, Task 4): `handleDiscard` now retries `deleteTournamentState` once on `{success:false}` and logs on double-failure, so a transient IO failure no longer orphans `.tournament_state.json`. _Original:_ The `3ad32bb` fix correctly prioritizes never trapping the app: if `deleteTournamentState` rejects (disk/IPC error), `discardBtn.onclick` logs and proceeds so `cleanup()` + `onAfterLeave()` always run. Consequence: the `.tournament_state.json` file then lingers, so the next folder-enter shows the resume (Continue / Start over) prompt for a tournament the user explicitly discarded. Recoverable via Start-over, and it requires an IO failure to trigger, so low severity — but the discard is silently incomplete. Improvement: on the next failed-delete or on startup, reconcile/retry the orphaned state delete (or surface a notification that discard couldn't remove the saved state). Effort: S. Affected: [tournament.js](../../tournament.js) (`handleDiscard`), [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`showTournamentLeavePrompt` discard path; resume-prompt entry). [from PR #58 fix-verification]
- [x] **`onAppCloseRequested` unsubscribe fn is discarded in `setupEventListeners`** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-07-02** (Group CW-T, Task 4): the returned cleanup is now stored in `this._removeAppCloseListener`. _Original:_ `window.electronAPI.onAppCloseRequested(...)` returns a `removeListener` cleanup (preload wraps `ipcRenderer.on`), but the renderer drops it, inconsistent with the established `onClipDownloadProgress` pattern (which stores + calls its cleanup). No runtime bug today — `setupEventListeners` runs once per `MediaViewer` and one instance per renderer process, so no accumulation — but it deviates from the CLAUDE.md gotcha ("`ipcRenderer.on()` listeners accumulate — use `.once()` or return a `removeListener` cleanup"). Scored 25 in review. Improvement: store the returned cleanup (call on teardown) or add a one-line comment documenting why cleanup is unnecessary here. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`setupEventListeners`, ~L1976). [from PR #58 review, scored 25]

Fix: update the assertion to the v2 history-free contract (expect `0`, or assert resumed state another way). Effort: XS-S. Both outcomes land in a safe state (the last gesture was X, so quitting is arguably correct) and it needs a deliberate double-gesture, so it's low severity. Fix: a re-entrancy guard in `handleAppCloseRequest` (e.g. an `isLeavePromptOpen` flag) that replies `allowAppClose()`/no-ops when a leave prompt is already showing. Effort: S. ### [2026-06-25] PR #55 post-merge review follow-ups (3 items)

**Origin**: `/code-review` pass on PR #55 (Group P2 — tournament large-folder perf; **merged** into `main` in `51366cb`). Five-agent review + per-issue confidence scoring. One finding scored 100 (initial tournament state written to a `null` folder on start — `handleStartClick` was changed to call `flush()`, which reads `this._persistFolder`, only ever set by `_schedulePersist()` and never on the start path, so the write went to `null`, `path.join(null,…)` threw in main, and `_drain()`'s catch swallowed it; the tournament wasn't persisted until the first pick) — **fixed in-branch pre-merge in `8420a7c`** with a regression test ([comment](https://github.com/GoodAlex223/media-viewer/pull/55#issuecomment-4796262458)), so the re-review posted "No issues found" ([comment](https://github.com/GoodAlex223/media-viewer/pull/55#issuecomment-4796654514)). The three items below are the sub-threshold tail, all the same root cause: PR #55's debounce migration converted three **structural-mutation** persistence points (not per-pick writes) from awaited-durable (`await _persistState`, established in `c6914ef`) to fire-and-forget debounced (`_schedulePersist`), each opening a narrow crash / immediate-exit window where the mutation is lost. Normal Save&Leave (`flush()`) and Discard (`cancelPending()`) paths are safe; only a crash or force-quit inside the debounce window loses the write, and reconciliation self-heals it on next resume (extra interrupt, not data loss). A 4th flagged item — `cancelPending()` not awaiting an in-flight write could "resurrect" a deleted state file (scored 75) — was assessed a **false positive**: Electron IPC is FIFO per renderer, so a write already in flight is processed before the subsequent `deleteTournamentState`, leaving the file deleted. All Claude-surfaced.

- [x] **Reconciliation repair persists via debounce instead of durably (`handleResumeReconciled`)** — ✅ **Superseded 2026-07-02** (Group CW-T bug #1): reconciliation is now factored into `reconcileWithFiles` and runs on **every** tournament entry (idempotent, via `_enterResumedTournamentUI`), so a debounced-write loss self-heals on the next entry — the stale-state-re-fires symptom is neutralized (the write remains debounced by design). _Original:_ (scored 75) — On resume with a file-set delta (files deleted from the folder since last session), the `if (removed.length > 0)` branch prunes `engine.files` and was changed from `await this._persistState(...)` to `this._schedulePersist(...)`. Reconciliation is a one-shot structural repair of the on-disk state file, not a per-pick write — if the user immediately picks Leave→Discard (`cancelPending()` drops the queued write) or the app crashes within the debounce window, the reconciled state is never written; on next open the stale file still lists the deleted files and the reconciliation warning re-fires. Fix: keep the reconciliation write durable (`await this.flush()` / `await this._persistState(...)`), debounce per-pick writes only. Effort: XS. Affected: [tournament.js](../../tournament.js) (`handleResumeReconciled`). [root cause shared with the two items below]
- [x] **Mid-tournament missing-file removal persists via debounce (`showTournamentPair`)** — ✅ **Superseded 2026-07-02** (Group CW-T bug #1): the `-1` branch is now effectively unreachable (reconcile-on-every-entry prunes absent files first), and if it does fire it re-reconciles on the next entry — the redundant-re-detection symptom is neutralized. _Original:_ (scored 50) — The `leftIdx === -1 || rightIdx === -1` branch calls `engine.removeFile(missing)` then was changed from `await tournament._persistState(...)` to `_schedulePersist(...)`. If the app closes/crashes within the debounce window before any later pick fires the write, the removal isn't persisted and the same file is re-detected as missing on next resume (repeated notification; self-heals via reconciliation, so the symptom is a redundant interrupt, not data loss). Fix: `await flush()` for this structural removal, or accept and document the trade-off. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`showTournamentPair` missing-file branch). [possible-dup-of: this group's `handleResumeReconciled` item — same root cause, different site]
- [x] **Special-move engine removal persists via debounce + stale "persist before navigation" comment (`moveToSpecialFolder`)** — ✅ **Resolved 2026-07-02** (Group CW-T, Task 4): took the "update the comment" option — the inline comment now reflects the intentional debounced schedule (a crash-window loss self-heals: the file is gone from disk, so resume reconciliation prunes it). _Original:_ (scored 50) — The tournament branch physically relocates a file on disk via IPC then removes it from the engine; the persist was changed from `await tournament._persistState(this.baseFolderPath)` to `_schedulePersist(...)`, but the inline comment still reads `// Tournament mode: also remove from engine + persist before navigation` — now misleading (the call is fire-and-forget, not awaited "before navigation"). A crash inside the debounce window leaves a state/disk mismatch (file moved on disk, still listed in `.tournament_state.json`) → an extra reconciliation prompt on next resume. Fix: either `await flush()` here (matching the comment) or update the comment to reflect scheduled persistence. Effort: XS. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`moveToSpecialFolder` tournament branch — comment + persist call). [possible-dup-of: this group's `handleResumeReconciled` item — same root cause, different site]

(3) **`engine.files` vs `strategy.files` diverge after `removeFile()`** — `engine.files` is authoritative for `getTierBreakdown()`/`handleApply()`; `recordResult()` captures `filesSnapshot: [...this.files]` and `undo()` restores it, because `strategy.serialize()` doesn't capture engine-level files removed between picks (else tier counts under-count after a removeFile→undo). So even after all three PRs land, the visual-similarity sort's graph-build wall-time is the floor. (The **AI-prediction sort has no graph build** — O(n) scoring + O(n log n) sort — so it is fully addressed by PR3 + PR1's progress; this gap is specific to vptree/mst/clip _similarity_ sorts.) The floor moves only via **(a) workstream #7** — parallelize the per-file `findKNearest` across a worker pool (same total work ÷ cores, quality-preserving; see the [2026-06-19] #7 entry below, already measure-first) — or **(b) relax the quality-lock to a bounded K-cap** (e.g. 64–128), the direct fix the user explicitly declined in PR1 ("quality must not change at all"; see [archived plan](../archive/plans/2026-06-19-sort-responsiveness-core.md) + spec §Decisions 2). Note PR1 already made this phase non-freezing/transparent/cancelable, so the decision is about raw wall-time, not freeze. **Decide after PR2/PR3 land + a measured 24k per-phase timing pass** (instrument per-phase timings during PR2/PR3 so the call is data-driven). ### [2026-06-20] PR #54 post-merge review follow-ups (2 sub-threshold items)

**Origin**: `/code-review` pass on PR #54 (Group P1 **PR1 of 3** — sort responsiveness core; merged into `main` in `7b78a56`). Five-agent review + per-issue confidence scoring; no finding scored ≥80/100, so the review posted "No issues found" ([comment](https://github.com/GoodAlex223/media-viewer/pull/54#issuecomment-4759718929)). The CLAUDE.md-compliance, shallow-bug, git-history, prior-PR-comment, and code-comment agents confirmed the dead-code removal is complete (no live callers), the `52f2cbc` abort-string fix is not regressed, and the cache-path algorithm threading is intact. The two items below are the genuinely-actionable sub-threshold tail. (See also the [2026-06-19] PR1 closeout follow-ups below — the optional progress-card E2E smoke item there covers the same `updateSortProgress` DOM path.)

- [ ] **Progress-card DOM teardown / flicker when `updateProgressNotification` interleaves with `updateSortProgress` during a concurrent sort** (scored 50) — `updateSortProgress` and `updateProgressNotification` share the single `this.progressNotification` element. If a non-sort caller (ML scoring, or `trainFromHistoricalRatings`, which calls `updateProgressNotification` in a loop) fires while a large-folder similarity sort is active — now possible precisely because PR1 made the sort non-freezing — `updateProgressNotification` does `innerHTML = ''` and rebuilds the simple-text form, tearing down the sort card's Cancel button + progress bar; the next `updateSortProgress` call rebuilds the card (guarded by `el.querySelector('.progress-phase')` being null). The PR's guard correctly prevents the null-deref / TypeError (no crash, no listener accumulation — the old Cancel listener dies with the removed node, abort still works), so this is a brief visual flicker + momentary Cancel-button unavailability in a rare race, not a correctness bug. Acknowledged as an accepted rough edge in the PR1 plan. Fix directions: a `{ signal: this.sortAbortController.signal }`-scoped Cancel listener with unconditional re-inject (per the CLAUDE.md AbortController-scoped-listener pattern), or separate DOM elements for sort vs non-sort progress. Effort: S. Affected: [media-viewer.js](../../media-viewer.js) (`updateSortProgress`, `updateProgressNotification`).
- [ ] **Document why `VPTree.findNearest`'s excluded-node ball-pruning is exact (and differs from `findKNearest`)** (scored 0 — false positive as a _bug_, filed only as a maintainability/doc-clarity improvement) — PR1 replaced the O(n²) MST linear-scan fallback with `vpTree.findNearest(current, traversed)`, justified at the call sites as "exact NN, same distanceFunc, excluding `traversed`". A reviewer flagged that `findNearest` applies standard VP-tree ball pruning even when the vantage point is in the `excludeSet`, whereas `findKNearest` searches both children unconditionally for excluded VPs — and worried the pruning could miss a closer non-excluded node. This was confirmed a **false positive**: VP-tree pruning depends only on the target↔vantage-point geometry (triangle inequality), not on whether the VP is an eligible result, and the `best.distance === Infinity` branch conservatively searches both subtrees until a valid candidate exists; the PR's 4 brute-force-equivalence tests confirm exactness. No code change needed, but a one-line comment in `findNearest` explaining why excluded-VP pruning stays correct (and why it can prune where `findKNearest` does not) would prevent a future reader from "fixing" it. Effort: XS. Affected: [sorting-worker.js](../../sorting-worker.js) (`findNearest`).
